stroke-linejoin="round" aria-hidden="true" class="size-3 shrink-0"><path d="M8.50001 6.827C8.14801 6.995 7.81801 7.225 7.52701 7.517L7.51701 7.527C6.13601 8.908 6.13601 11.146 7.51701 12.527L9.69201 14.702C11.073 16.083 13.311 16.083 14.692 14.702L14.702 14.692C16.083 13.311 16.083 11.073 14.702 9.692L13.771 8.761" stroke="currentColor" width="1.5" linecap="round" linejoin="round"></path><path d="M9.50002 11.173C9.85202 11.005 10.182 10.775 10.473 10.483L10.483 10.473C11.864 9.092 11.864 6.854 10.483 5.473L8.30802 3.298C6.92702 1.917 4.68902 1.917 3.30802 3.298L3.29802 3.308C1.91702 4.689 1.91702 6.927 3.29802 8.308L4.22902 9.239" stroke="currentColor" width="1.5" linecap="round" linejoin="round"></path></svg></div></a></div><span class="cursor-pointer">Fuzzy matching and substring search for full-text search</span></h3><span data-as="p"><a class="link" href="/guides/search/full-text-search">Full-text search</a> now supports two new ways to match text:</span><ul>
<li><strong>Fuzzy matching (typo tolerance)</strong> — In a <code>query_string</code> search, append <code>~</code> to a term to match indexed terms within a small edit distance: <code>body:(compxter~1)</code> matches <code>computer</code>. Use <code>term~</code> for an automatic distance based on term length, or <code>term~N</code> for a fixed distance (<code>N</code> is 0–2). Fuzzy works on existing indexes with no reindex. See <a class="link" href="/guides/search/full-text-search#query-syntax-reference">Fuzzy matching</a>.</li>
<li><strong>Substring search with n-grams</strong> — Configure a text field for character n-gram tokenization to match on partial words, prefixes, and autocomplete-style queries. Add an <code>ngram</code> object (<code>min_gram</code>, <code>max_gram</code>, <code>prefix_only</code>) to a field’s <code>full_text_search</code> config at index creation, then query it with ordinary <code>text</code> or <code>query_string</code> searches. See <a class="link" href="/guides/search/full-text-search#substring-search-with-n-grams">Substring search with n-grams</a>.</li>
</ul><span data-as="p">Full-text search remains in <a class="link" href="/release-notes/feature-availability">public preview</a> under API version <code>2026-01.alpha</code>.</span></div></div></div>

11.073 14.702 9.692L13.771 8.761" stroke="currentColor" width="1.5" linecap="round" linejoin="round"></path><path d="M9.50002 11.173C9.85202 11.005 10.182 10.775 10.473 10.483L10.483 10.473C11.864 linecap="round" linejoin="round"></path></svg></div></a></div><span class="cursor-pointer">Manage roles from your identity provider with SAML and SCIM</span></h3><span data-as="p">Organizations on the Enterprise plan with SSO can now manage Pinecone roles directly from their identity provider (IdP), instead of assigning them manually in Pinecone. Two modes are available:</span><ul>
<li><strong>SAML role management</strong> sets each user’s organization and project roles from a SAML attribute on every SSO login.</li>
<li><strong>SCIM provisioning</strong> syncs members and their roles in real time as you add, update, deactivate, or remove them in your IdP.</li>
</ul><span data-as="p">Both modes use the same <code>pinecone:*</code> role values and work with Okta and other SAML 2.0 / SCIM 2.0 providers. Configure them under <strong>Settings &gt; Access &gt; Identity provider</strong> in the Pinecone console. For more information, see <a class="link" href="/guides/production/configure-single-sign-on/okta-role-management">Manage roles with Okta</a> and <a class="link" href="/guides/production/configure-single-sign-on/okta-scim-provisioning">SCIM provisioning with Okta</a>.</span></div></div></div>

5.473L8.30802 3.298C6.92702 1.917 4.68902 1.917 3.30802 3.298L3.29802 3.308C1.91702 4.689 1.91702 6.927 3.29802 8.308L4.22902 9.239" stroke="currentColor" width="1.5" linecap="round" linejoin="round"></path></svg></div></a></div><span class="cursor-pointer">Expanded role-based access controls (RBAC)</span></h3><span data-as="p">Pinecone now offers an expanded set of roles for managing access to organizations and projects. New <strong>Organization member</strong> and <strong>Project member</strong> roles grant more restricted access than the owner and manager roles, and projects now support granular editor and viewer roles (project, control plane, and data plane) that you can assign to users, service accounts, and API keys.</span><span data-as="p">To better communicate the access hierarchy, organization owners are now shown in each project’s member list. This replaces the separate project-role assignments that could fall out of sync with the access organization owners inherit to all projects. Effective permissions are unchanged.</span><span data-as="p">If you used the previous role model, the <strong>User</strong> role has been renamed to <strong>Manager</strong>: the organization-level <strong>User</strong> role is now <strong>Organization manager</strong>, and the project-level <strong>User</strong> role is now <strong>Project manager</strong>. This is a name change only—permissions are unchanged and existing assignments are migrated automatically.</span><span data-as="p">You can assign and manage roles in the <a href="https://app.pinecone.io" target="_blank" rel="noreferrer" class="link">Pinecone console</a> or programmatically with the Admin API. For more information, see <a class="link" href="/guides/production/manage-rbac">Manage roles and access</a>, <a class="link" href="/guides/organizations/understanding-organizations#organization-roles">Organization roles</a>, and <a class="link" href="/guides/projects/understanding-projects#project-roles">Project roles</a>.</span></div></div></div>

stroke-linejoin="round" aria-hidden="true" class="size-3 shrink-0"><path d="M8.50001 6.827C8.14801 6.995 7.81801 7.225 7.52701 7.517L7.51701 7.527C6.13601 8.908 6.13601 11.146 7.51701 12.527L9.69201 14.702C11.073 16.083 13.311 16.083 14.692 14.702L14.702 14.692C16.083 13.311 16.083 11.073 14.702 9.692L13.771 8.761" stroke="currentColor" width="1.5" linecap="round" linejoin="round"></path><path d="M9.50002 11.173C9.85202 11.005 10.182 10.775 10.473 10.483L10.483 10.473C11.864 9.092 11.864 6.854 10.483 5.473L8.30802 3.298C6.92702 1.917 4.68902 1.917 3.30802 3.298L3.29802 3.308C1.91702 4.689 1.91702 6.927 3.29802 8.308L4.22902 9.239" stroke="currentColor" width="1.5" linecap="round" linejoin="round"></path></svg></div></a></div><span class="cursor-pointer">Cohere Rerank 4.0 Fast is generally available, and cohere-rerank-3.5 is deprecated</span></h3><span data-as="p"><a class="link" href="/models/cohere-rerank-4-fast"><code>cohere-rerank-4-fast</code></a> (Cohere Rerank 4.0 Fast) is now generally available as a <a class="link" href="/guides/search/rerank-results#reranking-models">reranking model</a> on Pinecone. It succeeds <code>cohere-rerank-3.5</code>, improves relevance quality, and supports multiple rerank fields. Cohere Rerank 4.0 is hosted on Azure AI under the Global Standard deployment type, and requests may be processed in regions outside the United States.</span><span data-as="p">With this release, <a class="link" href="/models/cohere-rerank-3.5"><code>cohere-rerank-3.5</code></a> is <strong>deprecated</strong>:</span><ul>
<li><strong>Through July 31, 2026</strong>: <code>cohere-rerank-3.5</code> continues to serve requests as before.</li>
<li><strong>August 1, 2026</strong>: requests to <code>cohere-rerank-3.5</code> are automatically served by <code>cohere-rerank-4-fast</code>.</li>
</ul><span data-as="p">No code change is required for the transition, but note two differences before you migrate:</span><ul>
<li><strong>Relevance scores differ.</strong> <code>cohere-rerank-4-fast</code> returns different scores than <code>cohere-rerank-3.5</code>. If your application relies on hard-coded score thresholds, re-tune them against <code>cohere-rerank-4-fast</code> before August 1.</li>
<li><strong>Billing counts units differently.</strong> The per-unit rate is unchanged at $2.00 per 1,000 rerank units. One rerank unit covers a query plus up to 100 documents, with documents longer than ~500 tokens auto-chunked into ~500-token chunks that each count toward that 100. As a result, <code>cohere-rerank-4-fast</code> can bill multiple units for a large request, whereas <code>cohere-rerank-3.5</code> billed one unit per request.</li>
</ul><span data-as="p">To control the transition yourself, migrate your rerank requests to <code>cohere-rerank-4-fast</code> before August 1. For model details and parameters, see <a class="link" href="/guides/search/rerank-results#reranking-models">Reranking models</a>.</span></div></div></div>

support on the Builder plan</span></h3><span data-as="p">The <a href="https://www.pinecone.io/pricing/" target="_blank" rel="noreferrer" class="link">Builder plan</a> now supports serverless indexes in every generally available cloud region, the same regions available on the Standard and Enterprise plans. Previously, Builder indexes were limited to the <code>us-east-1</code> region of AWS.</span><span data-as="p">Highlights:</span><ul>
<li><strong>All supported regions</strong> — Create Builder serverless indexes across AWS, GCP, and Azure, including <code>us-west-2</code> (Oregon), <code>eu-west-1</code> (Ireland), <code>eu-central-1</code> (Frankfurt), <code>ap-southeast-1</code> (Singapore), <code>us-central1</code> (Iowa), <code>europe-west4</code> (Netherlands), and <code>eastus2</code> (Virginia).</li>
<li><strong>Lower latency and data residency</strong> — Deploy indexes closer to your application or in a region that meets your data residency requirements, without upgrading to Standard or Enterprise.</li>
<li><strong>No pricing change</strong> — Builder remains a flat $20/month plan with no usage overages.</li>
</ul><span data-as="p">The Starter plan continues to support the <code>us-east-1</code> region of AWS only. For the full list of supported regions, see <a class="link" href="/guides/index-data/create-an-index#cloud-regions">Cloud regions</a>. For plan quotas, see <a class="link" href="/reference/api/database-limits">Database limits</a>.</span></div></div></div>

<li><strong>Templates and connectors</strong> — Start from pre-built templates, connect data sources (Google Drive, manual upload), and configure knowledge processing with the Knowledge Agent Toolkit (KAT).</li>

<li><strong>Versioning and rollback</strong> — Publish deployment versions and roll back to previous configurations.</li>
<li><strong>End-user experience</strong> — Authenticated chat interface with citations, visual components, and feedback collection.</li>
<li><strong>Increased Starter plan limits</strong> - The Starter plan is currently offering 1M input tokens per month (500K before promotion) to help explore Marketplace apps until June 30, 2026.</li>

xmlns="http://www.w3.org/2000/svg" width="18" height="18" viewBox="0 0 18 18" fill="none" stroke="currentColor" stroke-width="2" stroke-linecap="round" stroke-linejoin="round" aria-hidden="true" class="size-3 shrink-0"><path d="M8.50001 6.827C8.14801 6.995 7.81801 7.225 7.52701 7.517L7.51701 7.527C6.13601 8.908 6.13601 11.146 7.51701 12.527L9.69201 14.702C11.073 16.083 13.311 16.083 14.692 14.702L14.702 14.692C16.083 13.311 16.083 11.073 14.702 9.692L13.771 8.761" stroke="currentColor" width="1.5" linecap="round" linejoin="round"></path><path d="M9.50002 11.173C9.85202 11.005 10.182 10.775 10.473 10.483L10.483 10.473C11.864 9.092 11.864 6.854 10.483 5.473L8.30802 3.298C6.92702 1.917 4.68902 1.917 3.30802 3.298L3.29802 3.308C1.91702 4.689 1.91702 6.927 3.29802 8.308L4.22902 9.239" stroke="currentColor" width="1.5" linecap="round" linejoin="round"></path></svg></div></a></div><span class="cursor-pointer">Public preview: Full-text search</span></h3><span data-as="p"><a class="link" href="/guides/search/full-text-search">Full-text search</a> is now in <a class="link" href="/release-notes/feature-availability">public preview</a>. Full-text search uses a typed document model: you upsert data as JSON documents, declare ranking fields in a schema, and Pinecone indexes them accordingly. Schema field types: <code>string</code> with <code>full_text_search</code> (indexed for BM25 ranking), <code>dense_vector</code>, and <code>sparse_vector</code>. Any other fields you upsert are stored as metadata and automatically indexed for filtering — no schema declaration required.</span><span data-as="p">Highlights:</span><ul>
<li><strong>Four scoring methods</strong> via <code>score_by</code>: <code>text</code> (BM25), <code>query_string</code> (Lucene syntax, including cross-field boolean queries), <code>dense_vector</code>, and <code>sparse_vector</code>.</li>
<li><strong>New data plane endpoints</strong> under <code>/namespaces/{namespace}/documents/</code>: <code>upsert</code>, <code>search</code>, <code>fetch</code>, and <code>delete</code>.</li>
<li><strong>New filter operator</strong> <code>$match_phrase</code> for phrase matching against text fields, composable with any <code>score_by</code> method.</li>
<li><strong>Flexible deployment</strong>: on-demand read capacity (<code>read_capacity.mode: &quot;OnDemand&quot;</code>) and dedicated read capacity (<code>read_capacity.mode: &quot;Dedicated&quot;</code>) are both supported on managed (serverless) indexes.</li>
</ul><span data-as="p">Use API version <code>2026-01.alpha</code> to access the feature.</span></div></div></div>

1.91702 6.927 3.29802 8.308L4.22902 9.239" stroke="currentColor" width="1.5" linecap="round" linejoin="round"></path></svg></div></a></div><span class="cursor-pointer">Pinecone Assistant usage-based pricing and monthly Starter limits</span></h3><span data-as="p">Pinecone Assistant pricing is now fully usage-based. The hourly per-assistant fee has been removed. On Standard and Enterprise, you pay for what you use: ingestion (file uploads and updates on an assistant), storage, and chat, context, and evaluation tokens — with no base charge per assistant.</span><span data-as="p">For the Starter plan, Assistant included allowances are now monthly and reset each billing period (they are no longer all-time project totals). Starter includes <strong>500,000 chat input tokens, 300,000 chat output tokens, 500,000 context retrieval tokens, and 1,000 ingestion units</strong> per month. When you upload files to an assistant, usage is measured in ingestion units (approximately one unit per chunk, ~400 tokens); multimodal PDF chunks account for more ingestion units per chunk than standard text on the same meter.</span><span data-as="p">Per-assistant file count limits have been removed for all plans. Usage is governed by ingestion and storage allowances, file size and page limits, and rate limits instead of a cap on the number of stored documents.</span><span data-as="p">For details, see <a class="link" href="/guides/assistant/pricing-and-limits">Pricing and limits</a> and <a href="https://www.pinecone.io/pricing/" target="_blank" rel="noreferrer" class="link">Pinecone pricing</a>.</span></div></div></div>

8.761" stroke="currentColor" width="1.5" linecap="round" linejoin="round"></path><path d="M9.50002 11.173C9.85202 11.005 10.182 10.775 10.473 10.483L10.483 10.473C11.864 9.092 11.864 6.854 10.483 5.473L8.30802 3.298C6.92702 1.917 4.68902 1.917 3.30802 3.298L3.29802 3.308C1.91702 4.689 1.91702 6.927 3.29802 8.308L4.22902 9.239" stroke="currentColor" width="1.5" linecap="round" linejoin="round"></path></svg></div></a></div><span class="cursor-pointer">Metadata filter limit: 10,000 values per <code>$in</code>/<code>$nin</code> operator</span></h3><span data-as="p">Pinecone now enforces a limit of 10,000 values per <code>$in</code> or <code>$nin</code> operator in metadata filter expressions. This limit helps ensure consistent query performance and protects shared infrastructure from excessive load caused by very large filters.</span><span data-as="p">Requests that exceed this limit will fail with a <code>400 - BAD_REQUEST</code> error.</span><span data-as="p">If your application currently uses large <code>$in</code> filters (especially for access control), consider these approaches:</span><ul>
<li><strong>Namespace-based isolation</strong> (recommended): Create separate namespaces for each tenant instead of filtering by thousands of tenant IDs. This can also reduce query costs (queries on a 1 GB namespace cost 1 RU instead of 100 RUs for a 100 GB namespace with filtering).</li>
<li><strong>Access control groups</strong>: Filter by organization, project, or role identifiers instead of individual user IDs.</li>
<li><strong>Post-filter client-side</strong>: Retrieve a larger top K without filtering, then filter results in your application.</li>
</ul><span data-as="p">For more information, see <a class="link" href="/reference/api/database-limits#metadata-filter-limits">Metadata filter limits</a> and <a class="link" href="/guides/index-data/data-modeling#design-for-multi-tenancy">Design for multi-tenancy</a>.</span></div></div></div>

linejoin="round"></path></svg></div></a></div><span class="cursor-pointer">Request-per-second limits for data plane operations</span></h3><span data-as="p">Pinecone now enforces request-per-second rate limits on data plane operations (query, upsert, delete, and update) at the namespace level. These limits are set to 100 requests per second per namespace for all plans and provide protection against excessive request rates.</span><span data-as="p">Request-per-second limits are enforced in addition to existing read unit and write unit limits. If you exceed a request-per-second limit, you’ll receive a <code>429 - TOO_MANY_REQUESTS</code> error.</span><span data-as="p">For more information, see <a class="link" href="/reference/api/database-limits#data-plane-operations-requests-per-second-limits">Database limits</a>.</span></div></div></div>

href="/reference/api/latest/data-plane/fetch_by_metadata">Fetch by metadata</a> operation now supports pagination, allowing you to fetch large result sets in multiple requests. Use the <code>paginationToken</code> parameter to retrieve the next page of results.</span><span data-as="p">When there are more results available, the response includes a <code>pagination</code> object with a <code>next</code> token.
